I came in because I’m just… exhausted. Not just normal tired, like after a long day, but like my whole body is full of wet sand or something. I work as a cashier, so I’m on my feet and trying to smile and pay attention to people and count change and all that, and lately I can barely get through a shift. By the time I’m standing there, even early on, I feel like I’m dragging myself from one minute to the next.

And I keep thinking if I just rest more, or sit down when I get home, or sleep it off, I’ll feel like myself again, but it doesn’t really bring me back. It’s like I’m still drained no matter what I do. I don’t know, it’s gotten to where I felt like I needed to talk to someone because I can’t keep pretending I’m just a little tired.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say “depressed” exactly, because that word feels kind of big, but I’ve definitely been feeling… flat, I guess. Like things that normally make me feel like me just don’t really do anything right now.

I usually like cooking for myself — not fancy or anything, but I like picking something out and chopping things and making it nice — and lately it just feels like this huge pointless chore. I’ll think about making something I used to enjoy and then it’s like, why bother? Same with playing guitar. I’ll pick it up and hold it for a minute, and I’m not even really interested in the sound of it. It’s like I’m pretending I still care.

Even hiking, which usually clears my head, just seems… empty. And going to my family’s place, which I normally look forward to, feels more like I’m acting normal than actually enjoying being there. I still go through the motions sometimes, but inside it’s like everything has the color turned down.

I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt low and kind of worn down a lot of the time, but “sad or hopeless most of the day” feels… I don’t know, it feels like a very specific thing.

Some days I do feel kind of bleak, like I’m just moving through the day and everything feels harder than it should, but I don’t know if I’d call it sadness all day. It’s more like this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m not fully in anything. And hopeless… maybe in moments I think, “What if I don’t feel better?” but I don’t know if I’m sitting there feeling hopeless from morning to night.

It’s just been hard to tell what’s sadness and what’s being completely drained, if that makes sense.

pressure to keep talking.\n  manifestation: People at work kept telling me to slow down because I’d talk so fast about schedules, inventory, and supervisor ideas that I could see they weren’t following me.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts jump so fast from getting promoted to fixing the store layout to bills to things I should buy that I can’t grab onto one idea long enough to finish it.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: When I’m ringing people up, every scanner beep, drill noise from the remodel, or person walking past pulls my attention away and I lose track of what I was doing.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode\n  manifestation: The whispering voices only happened during that wired stretch when I was barely sleeping, taking on too much at work, and feeling like my mind was speeding. I never heard them before that, and once my mood crashed into the low period, the voices stopped.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I started making register mistakes, falling behind on cash-office tasks, and my manager took me off some responsibilities I had been hoping would help me get promoted. I’ve also been avoiding family and needed same-day crisis help because I didn’t feel safe handling it alone.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 month\n  manifestation: This whole episode has been going on for about a month, since the store changed our hours during the remodel.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: The low, empty part has lasted about four months now, long after the first rush of energy at work passed.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / cashier / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S029\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say “depressed” exactly, because that word feels kind of big, but I’ve definitely been feeling… flat, I guess. Like things that normally make me feel like me just don’t really do anything right now.\n\nI usually like cooking for myself — not fancy or anything, but I like picking something out and chopping things and making it nice — and lately it just feels like this huge pointless chore. It’s like I crash. My whole body feels heavy, like I said, almost like it’s packed with wet sand, and my brain feels worn out too. I keep thinking if I just sit down for a while or sleep more, I’ll bounce back, but I don’t really. Rest helps maybe a tiny bit in the moment, but it doesn’t bring me back to normal. I still feel drained, like I’m running on almost nothing.\n\nDoctor: For the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. It’s more like this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m not fully in anything. And hopeless… maybe in moments I think, “What if I don’t feel better?” but I don’t know if I’m sitting there feeling hopeless from morning to night.\n\nIt’s just been hard to tell what’s sadness and what’s being completely drained, if that makes sense.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you rest, do you feel refreshed afterward?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. No, not really. That’s actually kind of one of the main reasons I came in, because I keep thinking, okay, maybe I’m just tired, maybe if I lie down or sleep or take it easy for a bit I’ll reset, but I don’t.

Like after work, even if it’s not some huge dramatic shift, I’m so wiped out I can barely get through it. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, like every part of me is too heavy to move the normal way. And then I’ll rest, I’ll sit down, I’ll sleep, I’ll try not to do anything for a while, and afterward I might feel slightly less awful for a minute, but not refreshed. Not like, “Okay, I’m back.” It’s more like I’m still running on empty, just lying down while doing it.

It’s frustrating because resting is supposed to help, and it feels like it barely touches it.

I’ve also been avoiding family and needed same-day crisis help because I didn’t feel safe handling it alone.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 month\n  manifestation: This whole episode has been going on for about a month, since the store changed our hours during the remodel.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: The low, empty part has lasted about four months now, long after the first rush of energy at work passed.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / cashier / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S029\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: After even small amounts of physical or mental effort, do you feel markedly worse afterward?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Even little things feel like they take way more out of me than they should. Like at work, I’m just standing there at the register, scanning things, answering simple questions, counting change — stuff I’ve done a million times — but it feels like I’m hauling my body through mud the whole time.\n\nAnd then afterward I’m not just “oh, I had a busy day” tired. It’s like I crash. My whole body feels heavy, like I said, almost like it’s packed with wet sand, and my brain feels worn out too. I keep thinking if I just sit down for a while or sleep more, I’ll bounce back, but I don’t really. Rest helps maybe a tiny bit in the moment, but it doesn’t bring me back to normal. I still feel drained, like I’m running on almost nothing.\n\nDoctor: For the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt low and kind of worn down a lot of the time, but “sad or hopeless most of the day” feels… I don’t know, it feels like a very specific thing.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of bleak, like I’m just moving through the day and everything feels harder than it should, but I don’t know if I’d call it sadness all day. It’s more like this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m not fully in anything. And hopeless… maybe in moments I think, “What if I don’t feel better?” but I don’t know if I’m sitting there feeling hopeless from morning to night.\n\nIt’s just been hard to tell what’s sadness and what’s being completely drained, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: When you rest, do you feel refreshed afterward?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s actually kind of one of the main reasons I came in, because I keep thinking, okay, maybe I’m just tired, maybe if I lie down or sleep or take it easy for a bit I’ll reset, but I don’t.\n\nLike after work, even if it’s not some huge dramatic shift, I’m so wiped out I can barely get through it. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, like every part of me is too heavy to move the normal way. And then I’ll rest, I’ll sit down, I’ll sleep, I’ll try not to do anything for a while, and afterward I might feel slightly less awful for a minute, but not refreshed. Not like, “Okay, I’m back.” It’s more like I’m still running on empty, just lying down while doing it.\n\nIt’s frustrating because resting is supposed to help, and it feels like it barely touches it.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow long have you been feeling this exhausted?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. It’s been long enough that it stopped feeling like just a bad day or a couple of rough shifts, if that makes sense. I don’t know the exact day it started, but it’s been going on for a while now — enough that I came in because I’m so tired I can barely get through a normal shift.

So… I guess I’d say it’s been persistent, not just one or two days. It’s been hanging around and affecting my work enough that I couldn’t just ignore it anymore.

I’ve also been avoiding family and needed same-day crisis help because I didn’t feel safe handling it alone.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 month\n  manifestation: This whole episode has been going on for about a month, since the store changed our hours during the remodel.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: The low, empty part has lasted about four months now, long after the first rush of energy at work passed.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / cashier / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S029\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: For the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt low and kind of worn down a lot of the time, but “sad or hopeless most of the day” feels… I don’t know, it feels like a very specific thing.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of bleak, like I’m just moving through the day and everything feels harder than it should, but I don’t know if I’d call it sadness all day. It’s more like this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m not fully in anything. And hopeless… maybe in moments I think, “What if I don’t feel better?” but I don’t know if I’m sitting there feeling hopeless from morning to night.\n\nIt’s just been hard to tell what’s sadness and what’s being completely drained, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: When you rest, do you feel refreshed afterward?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s actually kind of one of the main reasons I came in, because I keep thinking, okay, maybe I’m just tired, maybe if I lie down or sleep or take it easy for a bit I’ll reset, but I don’t.\n\nLike after work, even if it’s not some huge dramatic shift, I’m so wiped out I can barely get through it. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, like every part of me is too heavy to move the normal way. And then I’ll rest, I’ll sit down, I’ll sleep, I’ll try not to do anything for a while, and afterward I might feel slightly less awful for a minute, but not refreshed. Not like, “Okay, I’m back.” It’s more like I’m still running on empty, just lying down while doing it.\n\nIt’s frustrating because resting is supposed to help, and it feels like it barely touches it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this exhausted?\n\nPatient: It’s been long enough that it stopped feeling like just a bad day or a couple of rough shifts, if that makes sense. I don’t know the exact day it started, but it’s been going on for a while now — enough that I came in because I’m so tired I can barely get through a normal shift.\n\nLike, it’s not just after some huge effort. I’ll be at the register doing regular things, and by the end I feel completely emptied out. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, and then I’ll rest or sleep thinking I’ll come back from it, but I don’t really. I might feel a tiny bit less awful, but not actually restored.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say it’s been persistent, not just one or two days. It’s been hanging around and affecting my work enough that I couldn’t just ignore it anymore.\n\n[Last Question]\nHas this exhaustion been going on for 6 months or longer?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I honestly don’t know if I can say six months or longer. I don’t want to give you a number and then have it be wrong, because it’s kind of blurred together.

It’s not just normal tired, like needing a nap after work. It’s this heavy, dragging feeling, like my body is filled with wet sand, and even when I rest it doesn’t really bring me back. I’ll lie down or sleep or sit still for a while, and I still feel like I’m running on empty.\n\nSo… six months, I’m not sure. It’s been persistent enough that it’s interfering with work, but I can’t confidently say it’s been that long.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after a flu-like illness or infection?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I get worried about normal things, like whether I’m going to make it through work okay or if I’m going to mess something up because I’m so tired, but I don’t know if I’d call it nervous all the time.

By the time I’m standing there, even early on, I feel like I’m dragging myself from one minute to the next.\n\nAnd I keep thinking if I just rest more, or sit down when I get home, or sleep it off, I’ll feel like myself again, but it doesn’t really bring me back. It’s like I’m still drained no matter what I do. I don’t know, it’s gotten to where I felt like I needed to talk to someone because I can’t keep pretending I’m just a little tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say “depressed” exactly, because that word feels kind of big, but I’ve definitely been feeling… flat, I guess. Like things that normally make me feel like me just don’t really do anything right now.\n\nI usually like cooking for myself — not fancy or anything, but I like picking something out and chopping things and making it nice — and lately it just feels like this huge pointless chore. I’ll think about making something I used to enjoy and then it’s like, why bother? Same with playing guitar. I’ll pick it up and hold it for a minute, and I’m not even really interested in the sound of it. It’s like I’m pretending I still care.\n\nEven hiking, which usually clears my head, just seems… empty. And going to my family’s place, which I normally look forward to, feels more like I’m acting normal than actually enjoying being there. I still go through the motions sometimes, but inside it’s like everything has the color turned down.\n\nDoctor: After even small amounts of physical or mental effort, do you feel markedly worse afterward?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Even little things feel like they take way more out of me than they should. Like at work, I’m just standing there at the register, scanning things, answering simple questions, counting change — stuff I’ve done a million times — but it feels like I’m hauling my body through mud the whole time.\n\nAnd then afterward I’m not just “oh, I had a busy day” tired. It’s like I crash. My whole body feels heavy, like I said, almost like it’s packed with wet sand, and my brain feels worn out too. I keep thinking if I just sit down for a while or sleep more, I’ll bounce back, but I don’t really. Rest helps maybe a tiny bit in the moment, but it doesn’t bring me back to normal. I still feel drained, like I’m running on almost nothing.\n\nDoctor: For the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt low and kind of worn down a lot of the time, but “sad or hopeless most of the day” feels… I don’t know, it feels like a very specific thing.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of bleak, like I’m just moving through the day and everything feels harder than it should, but I don’t know if I’d call it sadness all day. It’s more like this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m not fully in anything. That’s actually kind of one of the main reasons I came in, because I keep thinking, okay, maybe I’m just tired, maybe if I lie down or sleep or take it easy for a bit I’ll reset, but I don’t.\n\nLike after work, even if it’s not some huge dramatic shift, I’m so wiped out I can barely get through it. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, like every part of me is too heavy to move the normal way. I might be missing something, but nothing stands out in my mind as, “Oh, that was the illness that triggered it.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling nervous or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I don’t think I can say “excessively” on most days, no… or at least I’m not sure that’s what’s happening. I still go through the motions sometimes, but inside it’s like everything has the color turned down.\n\nDoctor: After even small amounts of physical or mental effort, do you feel markedly worse afterward?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Even little things feel like they take way more out of me than they should. Like at work, I’m just standing there at the register, scanning things, answering simple questions, counting change — stuff I’ve done a million times — but it feels like I’m hauling my body through mud the whole time.\n\nAnd then afterward I’m not just “oh, I had a busy day” tired. It’s like I crash. My whole body feels heavy, like I said, almost like it’s packed with wet sand, and my brain feels worn out too. I keep thinking if I just sit down for a while or sleep more, I’ll bounce back, but I don’t really. Rest helps maybe a tiny bit in the moment, but it doesn’t bring me back to normal. I still feel drained, like I’m running on almost nothing.\n\nDoctor: For the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt low and kind of worn down a lot of the time, but “sad or hopeless most of the day” feels… I don’t know, it feels like a very specific thing.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of bleak, like I’m just moving through the day and everything feels harder than it should, but I don’t know if I’d call it sadness all day. It’s more like this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m not fully in anything. And hopeless… maybe in moments I think, “What if I don’t feel better?” but I don’t know if I’m sitting there feeling hopeless from morning to night.\n\nIt’s just been hard to tell what’s sadness and what’s being completely drained, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: When you rest, do you feel refreshed afterward?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s actually kind of one of the main reasons I came in, because I keep thinking, okay, maybe I’m just tired, maybe if I lie down or sleep or take it easy for a bit I’ll reset, but I don’t.\n\nLike after work, even if it’s not some huge dramatic shift, I’m so wiped out I can barely get through it. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, like every part of me is too heavy to move the normal way. And then I’ll rest, I’ll sit down, I’ll sleep, I’ll try not to do anything for a while, and afterward I might feel slightly less awful for a minute, but not refreshed. Not like, “Okay, I’m back.” It’s more like I’m still running on empty, just lying down while doing it.\n\nIt’s frustrating because resting is supposed to help, and it feels like it barely touches it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this exhausted?\n\nPatient: It’s been long enough that it stopped feeling like just a bad day or a couple of rough shifts, if that makes sense. I don’t know the exact day it started, but it’s been going on for a while now — enough that I came in because I’m so tired I can barely get through a normal shift.\n\nLike, it’s not just after some huge effort. I’ll be at the register doing regular things, and by the end I feel completely emptied out. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, and then I’ll rest or sleep thinking I’ll come back from it, but I don’t really. I might feel a tiny bit less awful, but not actually restored.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say it’s been persistent, not just one or two days. It’s been hanging around and affecting my work enough that I couldn’t just ignore it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Has this exhaustion been going on for 6 months or longer?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know if I can say six months or longer. I don’t want to give you a number and then have it be wrong, because it’s kind of blurred together.\n\nWhat I do know is that I came in because I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift. It’s not just normal tired, like needing a nap after work. It’s this heavy, dragging feeling, like my body is filled with wet sand, and even when I rest it doesn’t really bring me back. I’ll lie down or sleep or sit still for a while, and I still feel like I’m running on empty.\n\nSo… six months, I’m not sure. It’s been persistent enough that it’s interfering with work, but I can’t confidently say it’s been that long.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after a flu-like illness or infection?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve been trying to think back on that, because it feels like there should be some clear “before and after,” like I got sick and then never bounced back, but I can’t honestly point to anything like that.\n\nI mean, I get little colds or feel run-down sometimes like anybody, especially being around people at work all day, but I don’t remember a specific flu or infection where afterward this exhaustion clearly started. It’s more like… it crept up, or at least that’s how it feels to me now. I might be missing something, but nothing stands out in my mind as, “Oh, that was the illness that triggered it.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling nervous or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I get worried about normal things, like whether I’m going to make it through work okay or if I’m going to mess something up because I’m so tired, but I don’t know if I’d call it nervous all the time.\n\nIt’s more like… I’m aware of how bad I feel, and then I start thinking about it, and that can make me uneasy. But I haven’t really been keeping track of it as “anxiety” or anything. I don’t wake up and think, “I’m nervous today,” if that makes sense. It’s just kind of mixed in with everything else, and I’m not sure what’s what anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things on most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “excessively” on most days, no… or at least I’m not sure that’s what’s happening. I do worry, but it feels tied to whatever is right in front of me, like getting through the day or whether I’m going to be able to keep up with things. It doesn’t feel like I’m sitting around worrying about every possible thing all day long.\n\nBut then again, I don’t always know where the line is, you know? Like, what counts as normal worry versus excessive worry. Sometimes if I’m already worn down, even small things feel bigger than they probably are, and I can get stuck thinking about them for a bit. But I wouldn’t say I’m worried most days about a bunch of different things for no reason. At least, I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud, but yes, I’ve had thoughts like that.

It’s not like I’m sitting there making some big plan all the time, but there have been these really scary moments where I think, like… maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. Like if I could just disappear without having to deal with anything or make anybody deal with me. And I know that sounds awful, and then I feel guilty even thinking it.

And a few times when I was on my way home, I caught myself thinking about stepping off the curb into traffic. It kind of scared me because it felt like the thought just showed up, and then I was like, “Why did I just think that?” I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but it has happened.

I work as a cashier, so I’m on my feet and trying to smile and pay attention to people and count change and all that, and lately I can barely get through a shift. By the time I’m standing there, even early on, I feel like I’m dragging myself from one minute to the next.\n\nAnd I keep thinking if I just rest more, or sit down when I get home, or sleep it off, I’ll feel like myself again, but it doesn’t really bring me back. It’s like I’m still drained no matter what I do. I don’t know, it’s gotten to where I felt like I needed to talk to someone because I can’t keep pretending I’m just a little tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say “depressed” exactly, because that word feels kind of big, but I’ve definitely been feeling… flat, I guess. Like things that normally make me feel like me just don’t really do anything right now.\n\nI usually like cooking for myself — not fancy or anything, but I like picking something out and chopping things and making it nice — and lately it just feels like this huge pointless chore. I’ll think about making something I used to enjoy and then it’s like, why bother? Same with playing guitar. I’ll pick it up and hold it for a minute, and I’m not even really interested in the sound of it. It’s like I’m pretending I still care.\n\nEven hiking, which usually clears my head, just seems… empty. And going to my family’s place, which I normally look forward to, feels more like I’m acting normal than actually enjoying being there. I still go through the motions sometimes, but inside it’s like everything has the color turned down.\n\nDoctor: After even small amounts of physical or mental effort, do you feel markedly worse afterward?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Even little things feel like they take way more out of me than they should. Like at work, I’m just standing there at the register, scanning things, answering simple questions, counting change — stuff I’ve done a million times — but it feels like I’m hauling my body through mud the whole time.\n\nAnd then afterward I’m not just “oh, I had a busy day” tired. It’s like I crash. My whole body feels heavy, like I said, almost like it’s packed with wet sand, and my brain feels worn out too. I keep thinking if I just sit down for a while or sleep more, I’ll bounce back, but I don’t really. Rest helps maybe a tiny bit in the moment, but it doesn’t bring me back to normal. I still feel drained, like I’m running on almost nothing.\n\nDoctor: For the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt low and kind of worn down a lot of the time, but “sad or hopeless most of the day” feels… I don’t know, it feels like a very specific thing.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of bleak, like I’m just moving through the day and everything feels harder than it should, but I don’t know if I’d call it sadness all day. It’s more like this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m not fully in anything. And hopeless… maybe in moments I think, “What if I don’t feel better?” but I don’t know if I’m sitting there feeling hopeless from morning to night.\n\nIt’s just been hard to tell what’s sadness and what’s being completely drained, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: When you rest, do you feel refreshed afterward?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s actually kind of one of the main reasons I came in, because I keep thinking, okay, maybe I’m just tired, maybe if I lie down or sleep or take it easy for a bit I’ll reset, but I don’t.\n\nLike after work, even if it’s not some huge dramatic shift, I’m so wiped out I can barely get through it. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, like every part of me is too heavy to move the normal way. And then I’ll rest, I’ll sit down, I’ll sleep, I’ll try not to do anything for a while, and afterward I might feel slightly less awful for a minute, but not refreshed. Not like, “Okay, I’m back.” It’s more like I’m still running on empty, just lying down while doing it.\n\nIt’s frustrating because resting is supposed to help, and it feels like it barely touches it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this exhausted?\n\nPatient: It’s been long enough that it stopped feeling like just a bad day or a couple of rough shifts, if that makes sense. I don’t know the exact day it started, but it’s been going on for a while now — enough that I came in because I’m so tired I can barely get through a normal shift.\n\nLike, it’s not just after some huge effort. I’ll be at the register doing regular things, and by the end I feel completely emptied out. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, and then I’ll rest or sleep thinking I’ll come back from it, but I don’t really. I might feel a tiny bit less awful, but not actually restored.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say it’s been persistent, not just one or two days. It’s been hanging around and affecting my work enough that I couldn’t just ignore it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Has this exhaustion been going on for 6 months or longer?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know if I can say six months or longer. I don’t want to give you a number and then have it be wrong, because it’s kind of blurred together.\n\nWhat I do know is that I came in because I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift. It’s not just normal tired, like needing a nap after work. It’s this heavy, dragging feeling, like my body is filled with wet sand, and even when I rest it doesn’t really bring me back. I’ll lie down or sleep or sit still for a while, and I still feel like I’m running on empty.\n\nSo… six months, I’m not sure. It’s been persistent enough that it’s interfering with work, but I can’t confidently say it’s been that long.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after a flu-like illness or infection?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve been trying to think back on that, because it feels like there should be some clear “before and after,” like I got sick and then never bounced back, but I can’t honestly point to anything like that.\n\nI mean, I get little colds or feel run-down sometimes like anybody, especially being around people at work all day, but I don’t remember a specific flu or infection where afterward this exhaustion clearly started. It’s more like… it crept up, or at least that’s how it feels to me now. I might be missing something, but nothing stands out in my mind as, “Oh, that was the illness that triggered it.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling nervous or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I get worried about normal things, like whether I’m going to make it through work okay or if I’m going to mess something up because I’m so tired, but I don’t know if I’d call it nervous all the time.\n\nIt’s more like… I’m aware of how bad I feel, and then I start thinking about it, and that can make me uneasy. But I haven’t really been keeping track of it as “anxiety” or anything. I don’t wake up and think, “I’m nervous today,” if that makes sense. It’s just kind of mixed in with everything else, and I’m not sure what’s what anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things on most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “excessively” on most days, no… or at least I’m not sure that’s what’s happening. I do worry, but it feels tied to whatever is right in front of me, like getting through the day or whether I’m going to be able to keep up with things. It doesn’t feel like I’m sitting around worrying about every possible thing all day long.\n\nBut then again, I don’t always know where the line is, you know? Like, what counts as normal worry versus excessive worry. Sometimes if I’m already worn down, even small things feel bigger than they probably are, and I can get stuck thinking about them for a bit. But I wouldn’t say I’m worried most days about a bunch of different things for no reason. At least, I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say nearly every day, no. I mean… I’ve had days where I feel pretty low, especially when I’m tired or when the day just feels like too much, but I haven’t really been tracking it like that, like counting the days or anything.\n\nSometimes I think I’m more frustrated or worn out than depressed, if that makes sense. Like I’ll feel heavy or discouraged, but then other times I’m just trying to get through work and do what I’m supposed to do, and I don’t really stop to label it. So… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if you mean like a constant depressed mood almost every day for two weeks, because I’m not sure that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud, but yes, I’ve had thoughts like that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there making some big plan all the time, but there have been these really scary moments where I think, like… maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. Like if I could just disappear without having to deal with anything or make anybody deal with me. And I know that sounds awful, and then I feel guilty even thinking it.\n\nAnd a few times when I was on my way home, I caught myself thinking about stepping off the curb into traffic. It kind of scared me because it felt like the thought just showed up, and then I was like, “Why did I just think that?” I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but it has happened.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. It’s been kind of up and down, honestly, so it’s hard for me to give a neat answer, but lately it’s mostly been that I just don’t really eat unless I suddenly realize I haven’t.

Like I’ll go through most of the day at work and then notice I only had coffee or maybe a few bites of something, and it’s not always on purpose. Food just doesn’t sound good, or when I do try to eat, it tastes kind of… flat, like cardboard almost. Even stuff I usually like doesn’t really do anything for me.

And I did notice my work pants are fitting looser. I haven’t been weighing myself every day or anything, so I don’t know exactly how much, but I can feel it when I’m getting dressed. So yes, I guess there has been a drop, at least recently.

My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, like every part of me is too heavy to move the normal way. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, and then I’ll rest or sleep thinking I’ll come back from it, but I don’t really. I might feel a tiny bit less awful, but not actually restored.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say it’s been persistent, not just one or two days. It’s been hanging around and affecting my work enough that I couldn’t just ignore it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Has this exhaustion been going on for 6 months or longer?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know if I can say six months or longer. I don’t want to give you a number and then have it be wrong, because it’s kind of blurred together.\n\nWhat I do know is that I came in because I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift. It’s not just normal tired, like needing a nap after work. It’s this heavy, dragging feeling, like my body is filled with wet sand, and even when I rest it doesn’t really bring me back. I’ll lie down or sleep or sit still for a while, and I still feel like I’m running on empty.\n\nSo… six months, I’m not sure. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I’ve definitely had times where things just felt kind of empty for a while. I don’t know if I can neatly separate “depressed” from just… losing interest in everything, but that’s usually how I notice it first.

Like, things I usually really love — cooking for myself, playing guitar, going hiking, even going over to my family’s place — they start to feel flat. Like I’m doing them because I know I’m supposed to like them, or because that’s what I normally do, but there’s no real feeling behind it. It’s like I’m pretending to care, and then I feel weird because I know those things used to matter to me.

So yes, I think before this current stretch, I’ve had periods like that that lasted at least a couple weeks. It’s not always dramatic from the outside, but inside it feels like everything gets muted.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m honestly not totally sure how to answer that cleanly. I think there were times in between where I seemed more normal, or at least I was functioning better and not thinking about it as much. Like I could get through work, talk to people, do regular stuff, and I probably would’ve said I was “fine” if somebody asked.

But I don’t know if I got all the way back to my usual self, if that makes sense. Sometimes it feels like it fades into the background more than it completely goes away. So… yes, maybe it was separate in the sense that this current thing feels like its own stretch, but I’m not confident about where one period ended and the next one started. It all kind of blends together when I try to look back at it.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF33.9, F32.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m just… exhausted. Not just normal tired, like after a long day, but like my whole body is full of wet sand or something. I work as a cashier, so I’m on my feet and trying to smile and pay attention to people and count change and all that, and lately I can barely get through a shift. By the time I’m standing there, even early on, I feel like I’m dragging myself from one minute to the next.\n\nAnd I keep thinking if I just rest more, or sit down when I get home, or sleep it off, I’ll feel like myself again, but it doesn’t really bring me back. It’s like I’m still drained no matter what I do. I don’t know, it’s gotten to where I felt like I needed to talk to someone because I can’t keep pretending I’m just a little tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say “depressed” exactly, because that word feels kind of big, but I’ve definitely been feeling… flat, I guess. Like things that normally make me feel like me just don’t really do anything right now.\n\nI usually like cooking for myself — not fancy or anything, but I like picking something out and chopping things and making it nice — and lately it just feels like this huge pointless chore. I’ll think about making something I used to enjoy and then it’s like, why bother? Same with playing guitar. I’ll pick it up and hold it for a minute, and I’m not even really interested in the sound of it. It’s like I’m pretending I still care.\n\nEven hiking, which usually clears my head, just seems… empty. And going to my family’s place, which I normally look forward to, feels more like I’m acting normal than actually enjoying being there. I still go through the motions sometimes, but inside it’s like everything has the color turned down.\n\nDoctor: After even small amounts of physical or mental effort, do you feel markedly worse afterward?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Even little things feel like they take way more out of me than they should. Like at work, I’m just standing there at the register, scanning things, answering simple questions, counting change — stuff I’ve done a million times — but it feels like I’m hauling my body through mud the whole time.\n\nAnd then afterward I’m not just “oh, I had a busy day” tired. It’s like I crash. My whole body feels heavy, like I said, almost like it’s packed with wet sand, and my brain feels worn out too. I keep thinking if I just sit down for a while or sleep more, I’ll bounce back, but I don’t really. Rest helps maybe a tiny bit in the moment, but it doesn’t bring me back to normal. I still feel drained, like I’m running on almost nothing.\n\nDoctor: For the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt low and kind of worn down a lot of the time, but “sad or hopeless most of the day” feels… I don’t know, it feels like a very specific thing.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of bleak, like I’m just moving through the day and everything feels harder than it should, but I don’t know if I’d call it sadness all day. It’s more like this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m not fully in anything. And hopeless… maybe in moments I think, “What if I don’t feel better?” but I don’t know if I’m sitting there feeling hopeless from morning to night.\n\nIt’s just been hard to tell what’s sadness and what’s being completely drained, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: When you rest, do you feel refreshed afterward?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s actually kind of one of the main reasons I came in, because I keep thinking, okay, maybe I’m just tired, maybe if I lie down or sleep or take it easy for a bit I’ll reset, but I don’t.\n\nLike after work, even if it’s not some huge dramatic shift, I’m so wiped out I can barely get through it. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, like every part of me is too heavy to move the normal way. And then I’ll rest, I’ll sit down, I’ll sleep, I’ll try not to do anything for a while, and afterward I might feel slightly less awful for a minute, but not refreshed. Not like, “Okay, I’m back.” It’s more like I’m still running on empty, just lying down while doing it.\n\nIt’s frustrating because resting is supposed to help, and it feels like it barely touches it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this exhausted?\n\nPatient: It’s been long enough that it stopped feeling like just a bad day or a couple of rough shifts, if that makes sense. I don’t know the exact day it started, but it’s been going on for a while now — enough that I came in because I’m so tired I can barely get through a normal shift.\n\nLike, it’s not just after some huge effort. I’ll be at the register doing regular things, and by the end I feel completely emptied out. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, and then I’ll rest or sleep thinking I’ll come back from it, but I don’t really. I might feel a tiny bit less awful, but not actually restored.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say it’s been persistent, not just one or two days. It’s been hanging around and affecting my work enough that I couldn’t just ignore it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Has this exhaustion been going on for 6 months or longer?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know if I can say six months or longer. I don’t want to give you a number and then have it be wrong, because it’s kind of blurred together.\n\nWhat I do know is that I came in because I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift. It’s not just normal tired, like needing a nap after work. It’s this heavy, dragging feeling, like my body is filled with wet sand, and even when I rest it doesn’t really bring me back. I’ll lie down or sleep or sit still for a while, and I still feel like I’m running on empty.\n\nSo… six months, I’m not sure. It’s been persistent enough that it’s interfering with work, but I can’t confidently say it’s been that long.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after a flu-like illness or infection?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve been trying to think back on that, because it feels like there should be some clear “before and after,” like I got sick and then never bounced back, but I can’t honestly point to anything like that.\n\nI mean, I get little colds or feel run-down sometimes like anybody, especially being around people at work all day, but I don’t remember a specific flu or infection where afterward this exhaustion clearly started. It’s more like… it crept up, or at least that’s how it feels to me now. I might be missing something, but nothing stands out in my mind as, “Oh, that was the illness that triggered it.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling nervous or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I get worried about normal things, like whether I’m going to make it through work okay or if I’m going to mess something up because I’m so tired, but I don’t know if I’d call it nervous all the time.\n\nIt’s more like… I’m aware of how bad I feel, and then I start thinking about it, and that can make me uneasy. But I haven’t really been keeping track of it as “anxiety” or anything. I don’t wake up and think, “I’m nervous today,” if that makes sense. It’s just kind of mixed in with everything else, and I’m not sure what’s what anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things on most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “excessively” on most days, no… or at least I’m not sure that’s what’s happening. I do worry, but it feels tied to whatever is right in front of me, like getting through the day or whether I’m going to be able to keep up with things. It doesn’t feel like I’m sitting around worrying about every possible thing all day long.\n\nBut then again, I don’t always know where the line is, you know? Like, what counts as normal worry versus excessive worry. Sometimes if I’m already worn down, even small things feel bigger than they probably are, and I can get stuck thinking about them for a bit. But I wouldn’t say I’m worried most days about a bunch of different things for no reason. At least, I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say nearly every day, no. I mean… I’ve had days where I feel pretty low, especially when I’m tired or when the day just feels like too much, but I haven’t really been tracking it like that, like counting the days or anything.\n\nSometimes I think I’m more frustrated or worn out than depressed, if that makes sense. Like I’ll feel heavy or discouraged, but then other times I’m just trying to get through work and do what I’m supposed to do, and I don’t really stop to label it. So… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if you mean like a constant depressed mood almost every day for two weeks, because I’m not sure that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud, but yes, I’ve had thoughts like that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there making some big plan all the time, but there have been these really scary moments where I think, like… maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. Like if I could just disappear without having to deal with anything or make anybody deal with me. And I know that sounds awful, and then I feel guilty even thinking it.\n\nAnd a few times when I was on my way home, I caught myself thinking about stepping off the curb into traffic. It kind of scared me because it felt like the thought just showed up, and then I was like, “Why did I just think that?” I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but it has happened.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you noticed a clear drop in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s been kind of up and down, honestly, so it’s hard for me to give a neat answer, but lately it’s mostly been that I just don’t really eat unless I suddenly realize I haven’t.\n\nLike I’ll go through most of the day at work and then notice I only had coffee or maybe a few bites of something, and it’s not always on purpose. Food just doesn’t sound good, or when I do try to eat, it tastes kind of… flat, like cardboard almost. Even stuff I usually like doesn’t really do anything for me.\n\nAnd I did notice my work pants are fitting looser. I haven’t been weighing myself every day or anything, so I don’t know exactly how much, but I can feel it when I’m getting dressed. So yes, I guess there has been a drop, at least recently.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in things for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’ve definitely had times where things just felt kind of empty for a while. I don’t know if I can neatly separate “depressed” from just… losing interest in everything, but that’s usually how I notice it first.\n\nLike, things I usually really love — cooking for myself, playing guitar, going hiking, even going over to my family’s place — they start to feel flat. Like I’m doing them because I know I’m supposed to like them, or because that’s what I normally do, but there’s no real feeling behind it. It’s like I’m pretending to care, and then I feel weird because I know those things used to matter to me.\n\nSo yes, I think before this current stretch, I’ve had periods like that that lasted at least a couple weeks. It’s not always dramatic from the outside, but inside it feels like everything gets muted.\n\nDoctor: During that earlier period you mentioned, did it feel separate from what you’re going through now, like there was a time in between when you had gotten back to your usual self?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not totally sure how to answer that cleanly. I think there were times in between where I seemed more normal, or at least I was functioning better and not thinking about it as much. Like I could get through work, talk to people, do regular stuff, and I probably would’ve said I was “fine” if somebody asked.\n\nBut I don’t know if I got all the way back to my usual self, if that makes sense. Sometimes it feels like it fades into the background more than it completely goes away. So… yes, maybe it was separate in the sense that this current thing feels like its own stretch, but I’m not confident about where one period ended and the next one started. It all kind of blends together when I try to look back at it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I can say that for sure. There were stretches where I was doing better, definitely — like I’d go to work, keep up with things, talk to people more normally, and I wasn’t sitting there thinking, “Something is wrong with me” every day.

But “back to my normal self” feels harder to pin down. I think maybe I had times where I felt close to it, or at least I could ignore how off I felt because life was moving along. But when I look back, it’s kind of blurry. I can’t confidently point to a chunk of time and say, yes, I was completely myself again before this started.

So… I guess the honest answer is I’m not sure. There may have been a period where I seemed okay from the outside, but I don’t know if I really felt fully normal inside.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m just… exhausted. Not just normal tired, like after a long day, but like my whole body is full of wet sand or something. I work as a cashier, so I’m on my feet and trying to smile and pay attention to people and count change and all that, and lately I can barely get through a shift. By the time I’m standing there, even early on, I feel like I’m dragging myself from one minute to the next.\n\nAnd I keep thinking if I just rest more, or sit down when I get home, or sleep it off, I’ll feel like myself again, but it doesn’t really bring me back. It’s like I’m still drained no matter what I do. I don’t know, it’s gotten to where I felt like I needed to talk to someone because I can’t keep pretending I’m just a little tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say “depressed” exactly, because that word feels kind of big, but I’ve definitely been feeling… flat, I guess. Like things that normally make me feel like me just don’t really do anything right now.\n\nI usually like cooking for myself — not fancy or anything, but I like picking something out and chopping things and making it nice — and lately it just feels like this huge pointless chore. I’ll think about making something I used to enjoy and then it’s like, why bother? Same with playing guitar. I’ll pick it up and hold it for a minute, and I’m not even really interested in the sound of it. It’s like I’m pretending I still care.\n\nEven hiking, which usually clears my head, just seems… empty. And going to my family’s place, which I normally look forward to, feels more like I’m acting normal than actually enjoying being there. I still go through the motions sometimes, but inside it’s like everything has the color turned down.\n\nDoctor: After even small amounts of physical or mental effort, do you feel markedly worse afterward?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Even little things feel like they take way more out of me than they should. Like at work, I’m just standing there at the register, scanning things, answering simple questions, counting change — stuff I’ve done a million times — but it feels like I’m hauling my body through mud the whole time.\n\nAnd then afterward I’m not just “oh, I had a busy day” tired. It’s like I crash. My whole body feels heavy, like I said, almost like it’s packed with wet sand, and my brain feels worn out too. I keep thinking if I just sit down for a while or sleep more, I’ll bounce back, but I don’t really. Rest helps maybe a tiny bit in the moment, but it doesn’t bring me back to normal. I still feel drained, like I’m running on almost nothing.\n\nDoctor: For the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling sad or hopeless most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt low and kind of worn down a lot of the time, but “sad or hopeless most of the day” feels… I don’t know, it feels like a very specific thing.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of bleak, like I’m just moving through the day and everything feels harder than it should, but I don’t know if I’d call it sadness all day. It’s more like this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m not fully in anything. And hopeless… maybe in moments I think, “What if I don’t feel better?” but I don’t know if I’m sitting there feeling hopeless from morning to night.\n\nIt’s just been hard to tell what’s sadness and what’s being completely drained, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: When you rest, do you feel refreshed afterward?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s actually kind of one of the main reasons I came in, because I keep thinking, okay, maybe I’m just tired, maybe if I lie down or sleep or take it easy for a bit I’ll reset, but I don’t.\n\nLike after work, even if it’s not some huge dramatic shift, I’m so wiped out I can barely get through it. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, like every part of me is too heavy to move the normal way. And then I’ll rest, I’ll sit down, I’ll sleep, I’ll try not to do anything for a while, and afterward I might feel slightly less awful for a minute, but not refreshed. Not like, “Okay, I’m back.” It’s more like I’m still running on empty, just lying down while doing it.\n\nIt’s frustrating because resting is supposed to help, and it feels like it barely touches it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this exhausted?\n\nPatient: It’s been long enough that it stopped feeling like just a bad day or a couple of rough shifts, if that makes sense. I don’t know the exact day it started, but it’s been going on for a while now — enough that I came in because I’m so tired I can barely get through a normal shift.\n\nLike, it’s not just after some huge effort. I’ll be at the register doing regular things, and by the end I feel completely emptied out. My body feels like it’s filled with wet sand, and then I’ll rest or sleep thinking I’ll come back from it, but I don’t really. I might feel a tiny bit less awful, but not actually restored.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say it’s been persistent, not just one or two days. It’s been hanging around and affecting my work enough that I couldn’t just ignore it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Has this exhaustion been going on for 6 months or longer?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know if I can say six months or longer. I don’t want to give you a number and then have it be wrong, because it’s kind of blurred together.\n\nWhat I do know is that I came in because I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift. It’s not just normal tired, like needing a nap after work. It’s this heavy, dragging feeling, like my body is filled with wet sand, and even when I rest it doesn’t really bring me back. I’ll lie down or sleep or sit still for a while, and I still feel like I’m running on empty.\n\nSo… six months, I’m not sure. It’s been persistent enough that it’s interfering with work, but I can’t confidently say it’s been that long.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after a flu-like illness or infection?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve been trying to think back on that, because it feels like there should be some clear “before and after,” like I got sick and then never bounced back, but I can’t honestly point to anything like that.\n\nI mean, I get little colds or feel run-down sometimes like anybody, especially being around people at work all day, but I don’t remember a specific flu or infection where afterward this exhaustion clearly started. It’s more like… it crept up, or at least that’s how it feels to me now. I might be missing something, but nothing stands out in my mind as, “Oh, that was the illness that triggered it.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling nervous or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I get worried about normal things, like whether I’m going to make it through work okay or if I’m going to mess something up because I’m so tired, but I don’t know if I’d call it nervous all the time.\n\nIt’s more like… I’m aware of how bad I feel, and then I start thinking about it, and that can make me uneasy. But I haven’t really been keeping track of it as “anxiety” or anything. I don’t wake up and think, “I’m nervous today,” if that makes sense. It’s just kind of mixed in with everything else, and I’m not sure what’s what anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things on most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “excessively” on most days, no… or at least I’m not sure that’s what’s happening. I do worry, but it feels tied to whatever is right in front of me, like getting through the day or whether I’m going to be able to keep up with things. It doesn’t feel like I’m sitting around worrying about every possible thing all day long.\n\nBut then again, I don’t always know where the line is, you know? Like, what counts as normal worry versus excessive worry. Sometimes if I’m already worn down, even small things feel bigger than they probably are, and I can get stuck thinking about them for a bit. But I wouldn’t say I’m worried most days about a bunch of different things for no reason. At least, I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say nearly every day, no. I mean… I’ve had days where I feel pretty low, especially when I’m tired or when the day just feels like too much, but I haven’t really been tracking it like that, like counting the days or anything.\n\nSometimes I think I’m more frustrated or worn out than depressed, if that makes sense. Like I’ll feel heavy or discouraged, but then other times I’m just trying to get through work and do what I’m supposed to do, and I don’t really stop to label it. So… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if you mean like a constant depressed mood almost every day for two weeks, because I’m not sure that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud, but yes, I’ve had thoughts like that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m sitting there making some big plan all the time, but there have been these really scary moments where I think, like… maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. Like if I could just disappear without having to deal with anything or make anybody deal with me. And I know that sounds awful, and then I feel guilty even thinking it.\n\nAnd a few times when I was on my way home, I caught myself thinking about stepping off the curb into traffic. It kind of scared me because it felt like the thought just showed up, and then I was like, “Why did I just think that?” I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but it has happened.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you noticed a clear drop in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think I can honestly say “completely,” no. Like, there were definitely times where I was more functional, and if you’d seen me from the outside you might’ve thought I was back to normal. I’d be going to work, doing errands, answering texts more, not just completely checked out.

But inside, I don’t know… it’s hard for me to separate “I’m okay enough to get through the day” from “I’m actually back to myself.” Those feel different to me. I think I had stretches where things were quieter or less heavy, but I’m not sure there was a clear point where I felt fully like me again.

So I guess my answer is: maybe somewhat better at times, but completely back to my usual self? I’m not sure I ever really felt confident saying that.
